Thus, finally, we deduce

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}|W(x)^{1/q}F(x)|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/q}\leq c_{1}K_{p}(c_{2}[V]_{A_{r(p)}}^{\alpha(p,q)})\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}|W(x)^{1/q}G(x)|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/q},

concluding the proof.
∎

8.
Appendix

Here we prove
Lemma

6.10
:

Lemma

6.10
.

Set

C_{n}:=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&n\end{bmatrix},\quad D_{n}:=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{1}{2n^{1/2}}\\
\frac{1}{2n^{1/2}}&\frac{1}{n}\end{bmatrix},\quad n=1,2,\ldots.

Then,
C_{n},D_{n}\in\nolinebreak\mathrm{P}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
with

\tr(C_{n}D_{n}),\penalty\ \tr(C_{n}^{-1}D_{n}^{-1})\simeq 1,

for all
n=1,2,\ldots
. Moreover, for all
a>1
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\tr(C_{n}^{a}D_{n}^{a})=\infty.

Before the proof, we note the identities

(8.1)

\begin{bmatrix}x&z\\
y&w\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{1}&0\\
0&\lambda_{2}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}x&y\\
z&w\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{1}x^{2}+\lambda_{2}z^{2}&\lambda_{1}xy+\lambda_{2}zw\\
\lambda_{1}xy+\lambda_{2}zw&\lambda_{1}y^{2}+\lambda_{2}w^{2}\end{bmatrix}

and

(8.2)

\tr\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&\lambda\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}x&z\\
y&w\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{1}&0\\
0&\lambda_{2}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}x&y\\
z&w\end{bmatrix}\right)=\lambda_{1}x^{2}+\lambda_{2}z^{2}+\lambda\lambda_{1}y^{2}+\lambda\lambda_{2}w^{2}.

Proof (of
Lemma

6.10
).

Let
n
be any positive integer. It is clear that
C_{n},D_{n}\in\nolinebreak\mathrm{P}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
. We compute

C_{n}D_{n}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{1}{2n^{1/2}}\\
\frac{n^{1/2}}{2}&1\end{bmatrix}

and

C_{n}^{-1}D_{n}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&\frac{1}{n}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{4}{3}&-\frac{2n^{1/2}}{3}\\
-\frac{2n^{1/2}}{3}&\frac{4n}{3}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{4}{3}&-\frac{2n^{1/2}}{3}\\
-\frac{2}{3n^{1/2}}&\frac{4}{3}\end{bmatrix},

showing immediately that
\tr(C_{n}D_{n}),\penalty\ \tr(C_{n}^{-1}D_{n}^{-1})\simeq 1
.

Next, we diagonalize
D_{n}
. Direct computation shows that it has the eigenvalues

\lambda_{1}(n):=\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{n}+\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda_{2}(n):=\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{n}-\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}}\right).

Notice that
\lambda_{1}(n)\simeq 1
and that

1-\lambda_{1}(n)=\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{n}-\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{-\frac{1}{n}}{1-\frac{1}{n}+\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}}},

therefore

|1-\lambda_{1}(n)|\simeq\frac{1}{n}.

A normalized eigenvector associated to
\lambda_{1}(n)
is given by
e_{1}(n)=(x(n),y(n))
, where

x(n):=\frac{\frac{1}{2n^{1/2}}}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{4n}+(\lambda_{1}(n)-1)^{2}}},\quad y(n):=\frac{\lambda_{1}(n)-1}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{4n}+(\lambda_{1}(n)-1)^{2}}}.

Observe that
|y(n)|\simeq\frac{1}{n^{1/2}}
. Let also
e_{2}(n)=(z(n),w(n))
be a normalized eigenvector associated to
\lambda_{2}(n)
.

Let now
a>1
be arbitrary. We have

D_{n}=\begin{bmatrix}x(n)&z(n)\\
y(n)&w(n)\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{1}(n)&0\\
0&\lambda_{2}(n)\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}x(n)&y(n)\\
z(n)&w(n)\end{bmatrix},

therefore

\displaystyle D_{n}^{a}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}x(n)&z(n)\\
y(n)&w(n)\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{1}(n)^{a}&0\\
0&\lambda_{2}(n)^{a}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}x(n)&y(n)\\
z(n)&w(n)\end{bmatrix}.

Thus, by (
8.2
) it follows that

\tr(C_{n}^{a}D_{n}^{a})=\lambda_{1}(n)^{a}x(n)^{2}+\lambda_{2}(n)^{a}z(n)^{2}+n^{a}\lambda_{1}(n)^{a}y(n)^{2}+n^{a}\lambda_{2}(n)^{a}w(n)^{2}\gtrsim_{a}n^{a-1}.

Thus,
\lim_{n\to\infty}\tr(C_{n}^{a}D_{n}^{a})=\infty
.
∎

Identities (
8.1
) and (
8.2
) suggest yet another family of counterexamples to the converse of the Cordes inequality. Let namely

C_{n}:=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&n(1+\log n)\end{bmatrix}

and

D_{n}:=\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{n}}&-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}&\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{n}}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&\frac{1}{n(1+\log n)}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{n}}&\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\\
-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}&\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{n}}\end{bmatrix},

for all
n=1,2,\ldots
. Then

(D_{n})_{11}\simeq 1,\quad(D_{n})_{22}\simeq\frac{1}{n},

therefore

\tr(C_{n}D_{n})\simeq 1+\log n,

for all
n=1,2,\ldots
. Thus,
\lim_{n\to\infty}\tr(C_{n}D_{n})=\infty
.

Let now
a\in\nolinebreak(0,1)
be arbitrary. Then, direct computation gives

(D_{n}^{a})_{11}\simeq_{a}1,\quad 0\leq(D_{n}^{a})_{12}=(D_{n}^{a})_{21}\lesssim_{a}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\quad(D_{n}^{a})_{22}\simeq_{a}\frac{1}{[n(1+\log n)]^{a}},

and

(D_{n}^{-a})_{11}\simeq_{a}1,\quad|(D_{n}^{-a})_{12}|=|(D_{n}^{-a})_{21}|\lesssim_{a}\frac{[n(1+\log n)]^{a}}{\sqrt{n}},\quad(D_{n}^{-a})_{22}\simeq_{a}[n(1+\log n)]^{a},

for all
n=1,2,\ldots
. It follows that for all
m,n=1,2,\ldots
, we have

\tr(C_{m}^{-a}C_{n}^{a}),\penalty\ \tr(C_{m}^{-a}D_{n}^{-a}),\penalty\ \tr(D_{m}^{a}C_{n}^{a})\simeq_{a}\max\left(1,\frac{n(1+\log n)}{m(1+\log m)}\right)^{a}

and

\displaystyle\tr(D_{m}^{a}D_{n}^{-a})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{a}\max\left(1,\frac{[n(1+\log n)]^{a}}{\sqrt{m}\cdot\sqrt{n}},\left(\frac{n(1+\log n)}{m(1+\log m)}\right)^{a}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{a}\max\left(1,\left(\frac{n}{m}\right)^{1/2},\left(\frac{n(1+\log n)}{m(1+\log m)}\right)^{a}\right).

Note that an elementary convexity argument gives

(1-\log 2)(x+y)(1+\log(x+y))\leq x(1+\log x)+y(1+\log y),

for all
x,y\in\nolinebreak(0,\infty)
with
x+y\geq 1
.
